Ephemeral values may only be used in certain contexts in Terraform configuration, and are not persisted to the plan or state files.\\n6:     - **`ephemeralasnull` function**: a function takes a value of any type and returns a similar value of the same type with any ephemeral values replaced with non-ephemeral null values and all non-ephemeral values preserved.\\n7: \\n8: BUG FIXES:\\n9: \\n10: - The `secret_suffix` in the `kubernetes` backend now includes validation to prevent errors when the `secret_suffix` ends with a number ([#35666](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/pull/35666)).\\n11: - The error message for an invalid default value for an input variable now indicates when the problem is with a nested value in a complex data type. Performance enhancements for resource evaluation, especially when large numbers of resource instances are involved ([#35558](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/issues/35558))\\n30: - The `plan`, `apply`, and `refresh` commands now produce a deprecated warning when using the `-state` flag. Instead use the `path` attribute within the `local` backend to modify the state file. ([#35660](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/issues/35660))\\n31: - backend/cos: Add new auth for Tencent Cloud backend ([#35888](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/pull/35888))\\n32: \\n33: UPGRADE NOTES:\\n34: \\n35: - backend/s3: Removes deprecated attributes for assuming IAM role. When used with DynamoDB-based locking, locks will be acquired from both sources. In a future minor release of Terraform the DynamoDB locking mechanism and associated arguments will be deprecated. ([#35661](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/issues/35661))\\n37: - `moved`: Moved blocks now respect reserved keywords when parsing resource addresses. ([#35850](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/issues/35850))\\n38: - config: In order to ensure consistency in results from HCL conditional expressions, marks must be combined from all values within the expression to avoid losing mark information. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_016M3qEUrRuupxcgovTUKV1T","type":"tool_result","content":"{\"content\":\"1: ## 1.9.0 (June 26, 2024)\\n2: \\n3: If you are upgrading from an earlier minor release, please refer to [the Terraform v1.9 Upgrade Guide](https://developer.hashicorp.com/terraform/language/v1.9.x/upgrade-guides).\\n4: \\n5: NEW FEATURES:\\n6: \\n7: * **Input variable validation rules can refer to other objects**: Previously input variable validation rules could refer only to the variable being validated. Now they are general expressions, similar to those elsewhere in a module, which can refer to other input variables and to other objects such as data resources.\\n8: * **`templatestring` function**: a new built-in function which is similar to `templatefile` but designed to render templates obtained dynamically, such as from a data resource result.\\n9: \\n10: ENHANCEMENTS:\\n11: \\n12: * `terraform plan`: Improved presentation of OPA and Sentinel policy evaluations in HCP Terraform remote runs, for logical separation.\\n13: * `terraform init` now accepts a `-json` option. If specified, enables the machine readable JSON output. ([#34886](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/pull/34886))\\n14: * `terraform test`: Test runs can now pass sensitive values to input variables while preserving their dynamic sensitivity. Previously sensitivity would be preserved only for variables statically declared as being sensitive, using `sensitive = true`. ([#35021](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/pull/35021))\\n15: * config: Input variable validation rules can now refer to other objects in the same module. ([#34955](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/pull/34955))\\n16: * config: `templatestring` function allows rendering a template provided as a string. ([#34968](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/pull/34968), [#35224](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/pull/35224), [#35285](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/pull/35285))\\n17: * core: Performance improvement during graph building for configurations with an extremely large number of `resource` blocks. ([#35088](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/pull/35088))\\n18: * built-in `terraform` provider: Allows `moved` block refactoring from the `hashicorp/null` provider `null_resource` resource type to the `terraform_data` resource type. ([#35163](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/pull/35163))\\n19: * `terraform output` with `cloud` block: Terraform no longer suggests that data loss could occur when outputs are not available. ([#35143](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/issues/35143))\\n20: * `terraform console`: Now has basic support for multi-line input in interactive mode. ([#34822](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/pull/34822))\\n21:     If an entered line contains opening parentheses/etc that are not closed, Terraform will await another line of input to complete the expression. This initial implementation is primarily intended to support pasting in multi-line expressions from elsewhere, rather than for manual multi-line editing, so the interactive editing support is currently limited.\\n22: * cli: Reduced copying of state to improve performance with large numbers of resources. ([#35164](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/issues/35164))\\n23: * `removed` blocks can now declare destroy-time provisioners which will be executed when the associated resource instances are destroyed. ([#35230](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/issues/35230))\\n24: \\n25: BUG FIXES:\\n26: \\n27: * `remote-exec` provisioner: Each remote connection will now be closed immediately after use. ([#34137](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/issues/34137))\\n28: * backend/s3: Fixed the digest value displayed for DynamoDB/S3 state checksum mismatches. ([#34387](https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/issues/34387))\\n29: * `terraform test`: Fix bug in which non-Hashicorp providers required by testing modules and initialised within the test files were assigned incorrect registry addresses. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_01CbEkfCcVcyjBgck1DvU4ZE","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_015MJJ31pKJ6oFU3kjcjsDrR","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/workspace/documentation.md","content":"# Terraform v1.9.0 to v1.10.0 Migration Guide\n\n## Overview\n\nTerraform v1.10.0 (released November 27, 2024) is a minor release that introduces new features for managing ephemeral data and contains important breaking changes that require attention when upgrading.\n\nThis guide covers the key breaking changes and new features you need to be aware of when migrating from Terraform v1.9.0 to v1.10.0.\n\n### Major Themes\n\n1. **Ephemeral Values and Resources** - New capability to handle sensitive data without persisting it to state\n2. **S3 Backend Restructuring** - Removal of deprecated IAM role assumption attributes\n3. **Improved Sensitive Value Handling** - Better consistency in mark propagation across conditional expressions\n4. **Moved Block Keyword Safety** - Improved resource addressing to avoid conflicts with reserved keywords\n\n---\n\n## Breaking Changes\n\n### 1. S3 Backend: Removal of Deprecated IAM Role Assumption Attributes\n\n#### What Changed\n\nTerraform v1.10.0 removes the following deprecated root-level attributes from the S3 backend configuration. These attributes must now be specified within the `assume_role` block.\n\n| Removed Attribute | New Location |\n|---|---|\n| `role_arn` | `assume_role.role_arn` |\n| `session_name` | `assume_role.session_name` |\n| `external_id` | `assume_role.external_id` |\n| `assume_role_duration_seconds` | `assume_role.duration` |\n| `assume_role_policy` | `assume_role.policy` |\n| `assume_role_policy_arns` | `assume_role.policy_arn` |\n| `assume_role_tags` | `assume_role.tags` |\n| `assume_role_transitive_tag_keys` | `assume_role.transitive_tag_keys` |\n\n#### Why This Changed\n\nThe consolidation into the `assume_role` block provides better organization and makes the configuration structure more consistent with Terraform's block-based syntax patterns.\n\n#### Before (v1.9.0)\n\n```hcl\nterraform {\n  backend \"s3\" {\n    bucket            = \"my-bucket\"\n    key               = \"terraform.tfstate\"\n    region            = \"us-east-1\"\n\n    # Deprecated attributes at root level\n    role_arn                     = \"arn:aws:iam::ACCOUNT_ID:role/MyRole\"\n    session_name                 = \"terraform-session\"\n    external_id                  = \"unique-external-id\"\n    assume_role_duration_seconds = 3600\n    assume_role_policy           = jsonencode({...})\n    assume_role_tags             = {\n      Environment = \"production\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n#### After (v1.10.0)\n\n```hcl\nterraform {\n  backend \"s3\" {\n    bucket = \"my-bucket\"\n    key    = \"terraform.tfstate\"\n    region = \"us-east-1\"\n\n    # New assume_role block structure\n    assume_role {\n      role_arn             = \"arn:aws:iam::ACCOUNT_ID:role/MyRole\"\n      session_name         = \"terraform-session\"\n      external_id          = \"unique-external-id\"\n      duration             = 3600\n      policy               = jsonencode({...})\n      tags                 = {\n        Environment = \"production\"\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n#### Migration Steps\n\n1. **Locate your S3 backend configuration** - Find the `terraform` block with `backend \"s3\"` in your root module (typically in `main.tf` or a dedicated `backend.tf`)\n\n2. **Create the `assume_role` block** - If you don't have one, add an empty `assume_role { }` block\n\n3. **Move deprecated attributes** - Move each deprecated attribute inside the `assume_role` block, using the new attribute names (e.g., `assume_role_duration_seconds` becomes `duration`)\n\n4. **Remove old attributes** - Delete the deprecated root-level attributes\n\n5. **Validate the configuration** - Run `terraform validate` to ensure the syntax is correct\n\n6. **Plan before applying** - Run `terraform plan` to ensure no state changes are planned before applying\n\n#### Common Pitfalls\n\n- **Forgetting to rename duration attributes** - Remember that `assume_role_duration_seconds` becomes `duration` (without the `assume_role_` prefix and `_seconds` suffix)\n- **Mixing old and new attributes** - Don't use both old root-level attributes and new block attributes simultaneously\n- **Case sensitivity** - Ensure you use the exact attribute names (e.g., `duration`, not `duration_seconds`)\n- **WebIdentity configuration** - If you use `assume_role_with_web_identity`, that block remains separate from `assume_role` and doesn't change\n\n---\n\n### 2. S3 Backend: New S3 Native State Locking\n\n#### What Changed\n\nTerraform v1.10.0 introduces experimental S3-native state locking as an opt-in feature. You can now use S3 Object Lock for state file locking instead of (or in addition to) DynamoDB.\n\n#### Why This Changed\n\nS3-native locking reduces dependencies on external services and integrates better with organizations that already rely on S3 bucket policies and object lock configurations.\n\n#### Important Notes\n\n- This feature is **experimental** and subject to change\n- When enabled, S3 locks are acquired **alongside** DynamoDB locks if both are configured\n- **Both locks must be successfully acquired** before operations proceed\n- S3 bucket and IAM policies need to be updated to allow the application to manage `.tflock` files\n\n#### Enabling S3 Native State Locking\n\n```hcl\nterraform {\n  backend \"s3\" {\n    bucket            = \"my-bucket\"\n    key               = \"terraform.tfstate\"\n    region            = \"us-east-1\"\n\n    # Enable experimental S3 native locking\n    use_lockfile = true\n  }\n}\n```\n\n#### S3 Bucket Policy Update Required\n\nWhen enabling `use_lockfile = true`, the lock file is stored alongside the state file with a `.tflock` extension. Update your bucket policy:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"Version\": \"2012-10-17\",\n  \"Statement\": [\n    {\n      \"Effect\": \"Allow\",\n      \"Principal\": {\n        \"AWS\": \"arn:aws:iam::ACCOUNT_ID:role/TerraformRole\"\n      },\n      \"Action\": [\n        \"s3:GetObject\",\n        \"s3:PutObject\",\n        \"s3:DeleteObject\"\n      ],\n      \"Resource\": \"arn:aws:s3:::my-bucket/terraform.tfstate*\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"Effect\": \"Allow\",\n      \"Principal\": {\n        \"AWS\": \"arn:aws:iam::ACCOUNT_ID:role/TerraformRole\"\n      },\n      \"Action\": [\n        \"s3:ListBucket\"\n      ],\n      \"Resource\": \"arn:aws:s3:::my-bucket\"\n    }\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n#### Migration Steps\n\n1. **Review your current locking setup** - Determine if you're using DynamoDB, S3-native, or no locking\n2. **Update bucket policies** - Add permissions for `.tflock` files if enabling S3 locking\n3. **Test in a non-production environment first** - Verify the configuration works before applying to production\n4. **Plan the transition** - If migrating from DynamoDB-only to dual locking, plan for the brief period where both are active\n5. **Monitor lock acquisition** - Watch logs to ensure locks are properly acquired from both sources\n\n#### Future Deprecation Notice\n\nIn a future minor version of Terraform, DynamoDB-based locking will be deprecated, and you'll need to migrate to S3-native locking or remove the DynamoDB configuration entirely.\n\n---\n\n### 3. Moved Blocks: Keyword Conflict Resolution\n\n#### What Changed\n\nMoved blocks now respect reserved keywords (`local`, `each`, `self`, `count`, `module`, `data`, `resource`, `terraform`, `variable`, `output`, etc.) when parsing resource addresses.\n\nIf you reference a resource whose type name matches a reserved keyword, you must now prepend `resource.` to the identifier in `moved` blocks.\n\n#### Why This Changed\n\nThis change prevents ambiguous resource references that could be interpreted as variable references or other language constructs. It ensures that moved blocks always reference the resource you intend.\n\n#### Before (v1.9.0)\n\nThis configuration would work if you had a resource type matching a keyword:\n\n```hcl\nmoved {\n  from = output.example\n  to   = aws_instance.example\n}\n```\n\n#### After (v1.10.0)\n\nYou must now use the `resource.` prefix for any resource type that matches a keyword:\n\n```hcl\nmoved {\n  from = resource.output.example\n  to   = aws_instance.example\n}\n```\n\n#### Resource Types That Require `resource.` Prefix\n\nThe following commonly-used providers have resource types that match reserved keywords:\n\n- `local_file` (type: `local`) - Actually safe, but ensure clarity\n- Custom providers with types named: `data`, `output`, `module`, `resource`, `variable`, `terraform`, `each`, `count`, `self`\n\n#### Migration Steps\n\n1. **Identify moved blocks in your configuration** - Search for all `moved` blocks in your configuration\n\n2. **Check resource type names** - For each `moved` block, verify if the resource type name matches any reserved keyword\n\n3. **Identify problematic references** - Run `terraform init` and `terraform plan` to see if any moved blocks produce errors about conflicting keywords\n\n4. **Add `resource.` prefix** - For any problematic references, prepend `resource.` to the resource reference in the `moved` block\n\n5. **Validate the configuration** - Run `terraform validate` to ensure all moved blocks are valid\n\n6. **Test the refactoring** - Apply the configuration to verify the moved blocks work correctly\n\n#### Common Examples\n\n**Example 1: Custom provider with resource type `local`**\n\n```hcl\n# v1.10.0 - If using a custom provider with \"local\" resource type\nmoved {\n  from = resource.local.example\n  to   = aws_instance.example\n}\n```\n\n**Example 2: Normal resources (no change needed)**\n\n```hcl\n# These don't need changes - \"aws_instance\" and \"aws_security_group\"\n# don't match reserved keywords\nmoved {\n  from = aws_instance.example\n  to   = aws_instance.renamed\n}\n```\n\n#### Common Pitfalls\n\n- **Not checking all moved blocks** - Search your entire codebase for moved blocks\n- **Forgetting about custom providers** - If you use custom providers, check their resource type names\n- **Applying prefix unnecessarily** - Only add `resource.` if the resource type actually conflicts with a keyword\n- **Ignoring error messages** - If Terraform reports a keyword conflict, add the prefix\n\n---\n\n## Sensitive Value Handling Improvements\n\n### What Changed\n\nTerraform v1.10.0 improves how sensitive marks are propagated in conditional expressions (`if/then/else`) and `for` expressions. In certain cases where v1.9.0 would lose sensitive marks, v1.10.0 preserves them for consistency.\n\n### Key Improvements\n\n1. **Conditional expressions now preserve sensitive marks** from all branches\n2. **For expressions preserve sensitive marks** when the result depends on sensitive inputs\n3. **Unknown values with sensitive marks** are now handled correctly\n4. **Bug fix: Incorrect transfer of sensitive marks** to nested resource values has been corrected\n\n### Why This Matters\n\nThese changes typically **improve security** by ensuring sensitive values remain marked throughout evaluation. However, you may see new \"sensitive value\" warnings in outputs that were previously hidden.\n\n### Before (v1.9.0) vs After (v1.10.0)\n\n```hcl\n# Example 1: Conditional expression with sensitive values\nvariable \"password\" {\n  sensitive = true\n  default   = \"secret\"\n}\n\nvariable \"use_custom\" {\n  type    = bool\n  default = false\n}\n\noutput \"chosen_password\" {\n  # In v1.9.0: This output might NOT be marked sensitive if use_custom is true\n  # In v1.10.0: This output IS marked sensitive because one branch contains sensitive data\n  value = var.use_custom ? \"custom-value\" : var.password\n}\n```\n\n### Migration Steps for Sensitive Value Changes\n\n1. **Run `terraform plan`** and review any new \"sensitive value\" annotations\n2. **Update outputs** - If outputs now show as sensitive and you want them public, use `nonsensitive()`\n3. **Review conditionals** - Check any `if/then/else` expressions that involve sensitive data\n4. **Use `nonsensitive()` carefully** - Only use this function if you've verified the value is safe to expose\n\n### Using `nonsensitive()` to Override\n\n```hcl\n# Example: Extract non-sensitive data from sensitive value\nvariable \"api_response\" {\n  sensitive = true\n}\n\noutput \"status_code\" {\n  # Extract just the status code, which is public information\n  value = nonsensitive(regexall(\"Status: (\\\\d+)\", var.api_response)[0][0])\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## New Features with Migration Implications\n\n### Ephemeral Values and Resources\n\nTerraform v1.10.0 introduces ephemeral values as a new capability for handling sensitive data that should not be persisted to state or plan files. This is a significant feature for secret management.\n\n#### What Are Ephemeral Values?\n\nEphemeral values exist only during a Terraform operation and are **not written to state files or plan files**. They're useful for:\n- Short-lived tokens and credentials\n- Temporary secrets from external services\n- Data that's only needed during apply but shouldn't be stored\n\n#### Ephemeral Input Variables\n\n```hcl\n# Define an ephemeral variable\nvariable \"api_token\" {\n  type      = string\n  ephemeral = true\n  # This variable won't be stored in state/plan files\n}\n\n# Use it in a provider configuration\nprovider \"example\" {\n  api_token = var.api_token\n}\n```\n\n#### Ephemeral Outputs (in child modules)\n\n```hcl\n# In a child module, define an ephemeral output\noutput \"temporary_credentials\" {\n  value     = aws_iam_session_token.example.token\n  ephemeral = true\n}\n```\n\n#### Ephemeral Resources\n\n```hcl\n# Define an ephemeral resource (provider-dependent)\nephemeral \"vault_generic_secret\" \"database_credentials\" {\n  path = \"secret/data/database\"\n}\n\n# Use it in another resource\nresource \"aws_db_instance\" \"example\" {\n  master_password = ephemeral.vault_generic_secret.database_credentials.data[\"password\"]\n}\n```\n\n#### Important Restrictions\n\n- Ephemeral values can **only** be referenced in:\n  - Other ephemeral contexts\n  - Provider configurations\n  - Provisioner and connection blocks\n  - Other ephemeral variables/outputs/resources\n\n- **Cannot** be referenced in regular resources or outputs\n\n#### The `ephemeralasnull()` Function\n\nNew in v1.10.0, use this function to replace ephemeral values with null while preserving non-ephemeral values:\n\n```hcl\n# Convert ephemeral values to null before storing elsewhere\nlocal {\n  safe_for_storage = ephemeralasnull(var.api_token)\n}\n```\n\n#### When to Use Ephemeral Values\n\n- **Provider authentication** - Pass credentials without storing in state\n- **Temporary secrets** - Use short-lived tokens from secret backends\n- **Sensitive defaults** - Provide sensitive default values that shouldn't be persisted\n- **Module outputs** - Return credentials from child modules without state persistence\n\n#### When NOT to Use Ephemeral Values\n\n- Regular resource attributes (use `sensitive = true` instead)\n- Data that needs to persist across `terraform plan` and `terraform apply`\n- State that must be available in other Terraform operations\n\n---\n\n## Testing Your Migration\n\n### Pre-Migration Checklist\n\nBefore upgrading to v1.10.0:\n\n- [ ] Backup your current state file\n- [ ] Review and document all moved blocks in your configuration\n- [ ] Review S3 backend configuration for deprecated attributes\n- [ ] Check for any custom providers with resource type names matching keywords\n- [ ] Test upgrade in a non-production environment first\n\n### Validation Commands\n\n```bash\n# Initialize with the new version\nterraform init\n\n# Validate configuration\nterraform validate\n\n# Plan to identify changes\nterraform plan -out=tfplan\n\n# Review the plan carefully\nterraform show tfplan\n\n# Apply only after reviewing\nterraform apply tfplan\n```\n\n### Key Validation Points\n\n1. **Moved blocks** - Verify no errors about keyword conflicts\n2. **S3 backend** - Confirm IAM role configuration is under the `assume_role` block\n3. **Sensitive values** - Check for any new \"sensitive value\" annotations\n4. **Plan result** - Ensure the plan shows no unexpected resource changes\n\n### Testing Sensitive Value Changes\n\n```bash\n# Run plan and grep for sensitive value indications\nterraform plan | grep -i sensitive\n\n# Compare before/after behavior with specific outputs\nterraform output -raw my_output  # Should fail if marked as sensitive\n```\n\n---\n\n## Rollback Guidance\n\n### If You Need to Rollback\n\nIf you encounter issues after upgrading to v1.10.0:\n\n1. **Revert your configuration changes** back to v1.9.0 syntax\n2. **Install Terraform v1.9.0**:\n   ```bash\n   terraform version  # Confirm you're on v1.10.0\n   # Download v1.9.0 from https://www.terraform.io/downloads\n   terraform version  # Confirm v1.9.0\n   ```\n\n3. **Run `terraform init`** to reinitialize with v1.9.0\n4. **Verify no state changes** with `terraform plan`\n5. **Contact Terraform support** if you identify a bug\n\n### Safe Rollback Strategy\n\n```bash\n# Step 1: Backup current state\ncp terraform.tfstate terraform.tfstate.v110.bak\n\n# Step 2: Revert configuration to v1.9.0 format\ngit checkout main -- *.tf  # or manually revert\n\n# Step 3: Switch to v1.9.0\ntfswitch 1.9.0  # or similar version manager\n\n# Step 4: Initialize with v1.9.0\nterraform init\n\n# Step 5: Verify no unintended changes\nterraform plan\n\n# Step 6: Contact support if plan shows unexpected changes\n```\n\n---\n\n## Specific Migration Examples\n\n### Example 1: Complete S3 Backend Migration\n\n**Before (v1.9.0):**\n```hcl\nterraform {\n  required_version = \">= 1.9.0\"\n\n  backend \"s3\" {\n    bucket                       = \"terraform-state\"\n    key                          = \"prod/terraform.tfstate\"\n    region                       = \"us-east-1\"\n    dynamodb_table               = \"terraform-locks\"\n\n    role_arn                     = \"arn:aws:iam::123456789012:role/TerraformRole\"\n    session_name                 = \"terraform-prod\"\n    external_id                  = \"abc123xyz\"\n    assume_role_duration_seconds = 3600\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**After (v1.10.0):**\n```hcl\nterraform {\n  required_version = \">= 1.10.0\"\n\n  backend \"s3\" {\n    bucket         = \"terraform-state\"\n    key            = \"prod/terraform.tfstate\"\n    region         = \"us-east-1\"\n    dynamodb_table = \"terraform-locks\"\n\n    assume_role {\n      role_arn     = \"arn:aws:iam::123456789012:role/TerraformRole\"\n      session_name = \"terraform-prod\"\n      external_id  = \"abc123xyz\"\n      duration     = 3600\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### Example 2: Ephemeral Variable Usage\n\n```hcl\n# Provider configuration var.vault_token\n}\n\n# Retrieve secrets using the ephemeral provider\ndata \"vault_generic_secret\" \"database\" {\n  path = \"secret/data/database\"\n}\n\n# Use secrets (but they won't be stored in state)\nresource \"aws_db_instance\" \"example\" {\n  master_username = data.vault_generic_secret.database.data[\"username\"]\n  master_password = data.vault_generic_secret.database.data[\"password\"]\n\n  # Note: These values ARE still stored in state as regular resource attributes\n  # Ephemeral only prevents the variable/data source from being persisted\n}\n```\n\n### Example 3: Moved Block with Keyword\n\n```hcl\n# If you had a resource with type \"local\" (hypothetically)\nresource \"random_pet\" \"pet\" {\n  keepers = {\n    example = \"value\"\n  }\n}\n\n# And previously had:\n# resource \"local\" \"old_name\" { }\n\n# In v1.10.0, update the moved block:\nmoved {\n  from = resource.local.old_name\n  to   = random_pet.pet\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Summary of Required Actions\n\n### Minimum Required Changes\n\n1. **S3 Backend Users**: Move IAM role attributes inside `assume_role` block\n2. **Moved Block Users**: Add `resource.` prefix if resource type matches keywords\n3. **All Users**: Run `terraform validate` and `terraform plan` before applying\n\n### Recommended Best Practices\n\n1. Use ephemeral variables for provider authentication instead of sensitive variables\n2. Review conditional expressions involving sensitive values\n3. S3 Backend: Removal of Deprecated IAM Role Assumption Attributes\n\n#### What Changed\n\nTerraform v1.10.0 removes the following deprecated root-level attributes from the S3 backend configuration. These attributes must now be specified within the `assume_role` block.\n\n| Removed Attribute | New Location |\n|---|---|\n| `role_arn` | `assume_role.role_arn` |\n| `session_name` | `assume_role.session_name` |\n| `external_id` | `assume_role.external_id` |\n| `assume_role_duration_seconds` | `assume_role.duration` |\n| `assume_role_policy` | `assume_role.policy` |\n| `assume_role_policy_arns` | `assume_role.policy_arn` |\n| `assume_role_tags` | `assume_role.tags` |\n| `assume_role_transitive_tag_keys` | `assume_role.transitive_tag_keys` |\n\n#### Why This Changed\n\nThe consolidation into the `assume_role` block provides better organization and makes the configuration structure more consistent with Terraform's block-based syntax patterns.\n\n#### Before (v1.9.0)\n\n```hcl\nterraform {\n  backend \"s3\" {\n    bucket            = \"my-bucket\"\n    key               = \"terraform.tfstate\"\n    region            = \"us-east-1\"\n\n    # Deprecated attributes at root level\n    role_arn                     = \"arn:aws:iam::ACCOUNT_ID:role/MyRole\"\n    session_name                 = \"terraform-session\"\n    external_id                  = \"unique-external-id\"\n    assume_role_duration_seconds = 3600\n    assume_role_policy           = jsonencode({...})\n    assume_role_tags             = {\n      Environment = \"production\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n#### After (v1.10.0)\n\n```hcl\nterraform {\n  backend \"s3\" {\n    bucket = \"my-bucket\"\n    key    = \"terraform.tfstate\"\n    region = \"us-east-1\"\n\n    # New assume_role block structure\n    assume_role {\n      role_arn             = \"arn:aws:iam::ACCOUNT_ID:role/MyRole\"\n      session_name         = \"terraform-session\"\n      external_id          = \"unique-external-id\"\n      duration             = 3600\n      policy               = jsonencode({...})\n      tags                 = {\n        Environment = \"production\"\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n#### Migration Steps\n\n1. **Locate your S3 backend configuration** - Find the `terraform` block with `backend \"s3\"` in your root module (typically in `main.tf` or a dedicated `backend.tf`)\n\n2. **Create the `assume_role` block** - If you don't have one, add an empty `assume_role { }` block\n\n3. **Move deprecated attributes** - Move each deprecated attribute inside the `assume_role` block, using the new attribute names (e.g., `assume_role_duration_seconds` becomes `duration`)\n\n4. **Remove old attributes** - Delete the deprecated root-level attributes\n\n5. **Validate the configuration** - Run `terraform validate` to ensure the syntax is correct\n\n6. **Plan before applying** - Run `terraform plan` to ensure no state changes are planned before applying\n\n#### Common Pitfalls\n\n- **Forgetting to rename duration attributes** - Remember that `assume_role_duration_seconds` becomes `duration` (without the `assume_role_` prefix and `_seconds` suffix)\n- **Mixing old and new attributes** - Don't use both old root-level attributes and new block attributes simultaneously\n- **Case sensitivity** - Ensure you use the exact attribute names (e.g., `duration`, not `duration_seconds`)\n- **WebIdentity configuration** - If you use `assume_role_with_web_identity`, that block remains separate from `assume_role` and doesn't change\n\n---\n\n### 2. S3 Backend: New S3 Native State Locking\n\n#### What Changed\n\nTerraform v1.10.0 introduces experimental S3-native state locking as an opt-in feature. You can now use S3 Object Lock for state file locking instead of (or in addition to) DynamoDB.\n\n#### Why This Changed\n\nS3-native locking reduces dependencies on external services and integrates better with organizations that already rely on S3 bucket policies and object lock configurations.\n\n#### Important Notes\n\n- This feature is **experimental** and subject to change\n- When enabled, S3 locks are acquired **alongside** DynamoDB locks if both are configured\n- **Both locks must be successfully acquired** before operations proceed\n- S3 bucket and IAM policies need to be updated to allow the application to manage `.tflock` files\n\n#### Enabling S3 Native State Locking\n\n```hcl\nterraform {\n  backend \"s3\" {\n    bucket            = \"my-bucket\"\n    key               = \"terraform.tfstate\"\n    region            = \"us-east-1\"\n\n    # Enable experimental S3 native locking\n    use_lockfile = true\n  }\n}\n```\n\n#### S3 Bucket Policy Update Required\n\nWhen enabling `use_lockfile = true`, the lock file is stored alongside the state file with a `.tflock` extension. Update your bucket policy:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"Version\": \"2012-10-17\",\n  \"Statement\": [\n    {\n      \"Effect\": \"Allow\",\n      \"Principal\": {\n        \"AWS\": \"arn:aws:iam::ACCOUNT_ID:role/TerraformRole\"\n      },\n      \"Action\": [\n        \"s3:GetObject\",\n        \"s3:PutObject\",\n        \"s3:DeleteObject\"\n      ],\n      \"Resource\": \"arn:aws:s3:::my-bucket/terraform.tfstate*\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"Effect\": \"Allow\",\n      \"Principal\": {\n        \"AWS\": \"arn:aws:iam::ACCOUNT_ID:role/TerraformRole\"\n      },\n      \"Action\": [\n        \"s3:ListBucket\"\n      ],\n      \"Resource\": \"arn:aws:s3:::my-bucket\"\n    }\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n#### Migration Steps\n\n1. **Review your current locking setup** - Determine if you're using DynamoDB, S3-native, or no locking\n2. **Update bucket policies** - Add permissions for `.tflock` files if enabling S3 locking\n3. **Test in a non-production environment first** - Verify the configuration works before applying to production\n4. **Plan the transition** - If migrating from DynamoDB-only to dual locking, plan for the brief period where both are active\n5. **Monitor lock acquisition** - Watch logs to ensure locks are properly acquired from both sources\n\n#### Future Deprecation Notice\n\nIn a future minor version of Terraform, DynamoDB-based locking will be deprecated, and you'll need to migrate to S3-native locking or remove the DynamoDB configuration entirely.\n\n---\n\n### 3. Moved Blocks: Keyword Conflict Resolution\n\n#### What Changed\n\nMoved blocks now respect reserved keywords (`local`, `each`, `self`, `count`, `module`, `data`, `resource`, `terraform`, `variable`, `output`, etc.) when parsing resource addresses.\n\nIf you reference a resource whose type name matches a reserved keyword, you must now prepend `resource.` to the identifier in `moved` blocks.\n\n#### Why This Changed\n\nThis change prevents ambiguous resource references that could be interpreted as variable references or other language constructs. It ensures that moved blocks always reference the resource you intend.\n\n#### Before (v1.9.0)\n\nThis configuration would work if you had a resource type matching a keyword:\n\n```hcl\nmoved {\n  from = output.example\n  to   = aws_instance.example\n}\n```\n\n#### After (v1.10.0)\n\nYou must now use the `resource.` prefix for any resource type that matches a keyword:\n\n```hcl\nmoved {\n  from = resource.output.example\n  to   = aws_instance.example\n}\n```\n\n#### Resource Types That Require `resource.` Prefix\n\nThe following commonly-used providers have resource types that match reserved keywords:\n\n- `local_file` (type: `local`) - Actually safe, but ensure clarity\n- Custom providers with types named: `data`, `output`, `module`, `resource`, `variable`, `terraform`, `each`, `count`, `self`\n\n#### Migration Steps\n\n1. **Identify moved blocks in your configuration** - Search for all `moved` blocks in your configuration\n\n2. **Check resource type names** - For each `moved` block, verify if the resource type name matches any reserved keyword\n\n3. **Identify problematic references** - Run `terraform init` and `terraform plan` to see if any moved blocks produce errors about conflicting keywords\n\n4. **Add `resource.` prefix** - For any problematic references, prepend `resource.` to the resource reference in the `moved` block\n\n5. **Validate the configuration** - Run `terraform validate` to ensure all moved blocks are valid\n\n6. **Test the refactoring** - Apply the configuration to verify the moved blocks work correctly\n\n#### Common Examples\n\n**Example 1: Custom provider with resource type `local`**\n\n```hcl\n# v1.10.0 - If using a custom provider with \"local\" resource type\nmoved {\n  from = resource.local.example\n  to   = aws_instance.example\n}\n```\n\n**Example 2: Normal resources (no change needed)**\n\n```hcl\n# These don't need changes - \"aws_instance\" and \"aws_security_group\"\n# don't match reserved keywords\nmoved {\n  from = aws_instance.example\n  to   = aws_instance.renamed\n}\n```\n\n#### Common Pitfalls\n\n- **Not checking all moved blocks** - Search your entire codebase for moved blocks\n- **Forgetting about custom providers** - If you use custom providers, check their resource type names\n- **Applying prefix unnecessarily** - Only add `resource.` if the resource type actually conflicts with a keyword\n- **Ignoring error messages** - If Terraform reports a keyword conflict, add the prefix\n\n---\n\n## Sensitive Value Handling Improvements\n\n### What Changed\n\nTerraform v1.10.0 improves how sensitive marks are propagated in conditional expressions (`if/then/else`) and `for` expressions. In certain cases where v1.9.0 would lose sensitive marks, v1.10.0 preserves them for consistency.\n\n### Key Improvements\n\n1. **Conditional expressions now preserve sensitive marks** from all branches\n2. **For expressions preserve sensitive marks** when the result depends on sensitive inputs\n3. **Unknown values with sensitive marks** are now handled correctly\n4. **Bug fix: Incorrect transfer of sensitive marks** to nested resource values has been corrected\n\n### Why This Matters\n\nThese changes typically **improve security** by ensuring sensitive values remain marked throughout evaluation. However, you may see new \"sensitive value\" warnings in outputs that were previously hidden.\n\n### Before (v1.9.0) vs After (v1.10.0)\n\n```hcl\n# Example 1: Conditional expression with sensitive values\nvariable \"password\" {\n  sensitive = true\n  default   = \"secret\"\n}\n\nvariable \"use_custom\" {\n  type    = bool\n  default = false\n}\n\noutput \"chosen_password\" {\n  # In v1.9.0: This output might NOT be marked sensitive if use_custom is true\n  # In v1.10.0: This output IS marked sensitive because one branch contains sensitive data\n  value = var.use_custom ? \"custom-value\" : var.password\n}\n```\n\n### Migration Steps for Sensitive Value Changes\n\n1. **Run `terraform plan`** and review any new \"sensitive value\" annotations\n2. **Update outputs** - If outputs now show as sensitive and you want them public, use `nonsensitive()`\n3. **Review conditionals** - Check any `if/then/else` expressions that involve sensitive data\n4. **Use `nonsensitive()` carefully** - Only use this function if you've verified the value is safe to expose\n\n### Using `nonsensitive()` to Override\n\n```hcl\n# Example: Extract non-sensitive data from sensitive value\nvariable \"api_response\" {\n  sensitive = true\n}\n\noutput \"status_code\" {\n  # Extract just the status code, which is public information\n  value = nonsensitive(regexall(\"Status: (\\\\d+)\", var.api_response)[0][0])\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## New Features with Migration Implications\n\n### Ephemeral Values and Resources\n\nTerraform v1.10.0 introduces ephemeral values as a new capability for handling sensitive data that should not be persisted to state or plan files. This is a significant feature for secret management.\n\n#### What Are Ephemeral Values?\n\nEphemeral values exist only during a Terraform operation and are **not written to state files or plan files**. They're useful for:\n- Short-lived tokens and credentials\n- Temporary secrets from external services\n- Data that's only needed during apply but shouldn't be stored\n\n#### Ephemeral Input Variables\n\n```hcl\n# Define an ephemeral variable\nvariable \"api_token\" {\n  type      = string\n  ephemeral = true\n  # This variable won't be stored in state/plan files\n}\n\n# Use it in a provider configuration\nprovider \"example\" {\n  api_token = var.api_token\n}\n```\n\n#### Ephemeral Outputs (in child modules)\n\n```hcl\n# In a child module, define an ephemeral output\noutput \"temporary_credentials\" {\n  value     = aws_iam_session_token.example.token\n  ephemeral = true\n}\n```\n\n#### Ephemeral Resources\n\n```hcl\n# Define an ephemeral resource (provider-dependent)\nephemeral \"vault_generic_secret\" \"database_credentials\" {\n  path = \"secret/data/database\"\n}\n\n# Use it in another resource\nresource \"aws_db_instance\" \"example\" {\n  master_password = ephemeral.vault_generic_secret.database_credentials.data[\"password\"]\n}\n```\n\n#### Important Restrictions\n\n- Ephemeral values can **only** be referenced in:\n  - Other ephemeral contexts\n  - Provider configurations\n  - Provisioner and connection blocks\n  - Other ephemeral variables/outputs/resources\n\n- **Cannot** be referenced in regular resources or outputs\n\n#### The `ephemeralasnull()` Function\n\nNew in v1.10.0, use this function to replace ephemeral values with null while preserving non-ephemeral values:\n\n```hcl\n# Convert ephemeral values to null before storing elsewhere\nlocal {\n  safe_for_storage = ephemeralasnull(var.api_token)\n}\n```\n\n#### When to Use Ephemeral Values\n\n- **Provider authentication** - Pass credentials without storing in state\n- **Temporary secrets** - Use short-lived tokens from secret backends\n- **Sensitive defaults** - Provide sensitive default values that shouldn't be persisted\n- **Module outputs** - Return credentials from child modules without state persistence\n\n#### When NOT to Use Ephemeral Values\n\n- Regular resource attributes (use `sensitive = true` instead)\n- Data that needs to persist across `terraform plan` and `terraform apply`\n- State that must be available in other Terraform operations\n\n---\n\n## Testing Your Migration\n\n### Pre-Migration Checklist\n\nBefore upgrading to v1.10.0:\n\n- [ ] Backup your current state file\n- [ ] Review and document all moved blocks in your configuration\n- [ ] Review S3 backend configuration for deprecated attributes\n- [ ] Check for any custom providers with resource type names matching keywords\n- [ ] Test upgrade in a non-production environment first\n\n### Validation Commands\n\n```bash\n# Initialize with the new version\nterraform init\n\n# Validate configuration\nterraform validate\n\n# Plan to identify changes\nterraform plan -out=tfplan\n\n# Review the plan carefully\nterraform show tfplan\n\n# Apply only after reviewing\nterraform apply tfplan\n```\n\n### Key Validation Points\n\n1. **Moved blocks** - Verify no errors about keyword conflicts\n2. **S3 backend** - Confirm IAM role configuration is under the `assume_role` block\n3. **Sensitive values** - Check for any new \"sensitive value\" annotations\n4. **Plan result** - Ensure the plan shows no unexpected resource changes\n\n### Testing Sensitive Value Changes\n\n```bash\n# Run plan and grep for sensitive value indications\nterraform plan | grep -i sensitive\n\n# Compare before/after behavior with specific outputs\nterraform output -raw my_output  # Should fail if marked as sensitive\n```\n\n---\n\n## Rollback Guidance\n\n### If You Need to Rollback\n\nIf you encounter issues after upgrading to v1.10.0:\n\n1. **Revert your configuration changes** back to v1.9.0 syntax\n2. **Install Terraform v1.9.0**:\n   ```bash\n   terraform version  # Confirm you're on v1.10.0\n   # Download v1.9.0 from https://www.terraform.io/downloads\n   terraform version  # Confirm v1.9.0\n   ```\n\n3. **Run `terraform init`** to reinitialize with v1.9.0\n4. **Verify no state changes** with `terraform plan`\n5. **Contact Terraform support** if you identify a bug\n\n### Safe Rollback Strategy\n\n```bash\n# Step 1: Backup current state\ncp terraform.tfstate terraform.tfstate.v110.bak\n\n# Step 2: Revert configuration to v1.9.0 format\ngit checkout main -- *.tf  # or manually revert\n\n# Step 3: Switch to v1.9.0\ntfswitch 1.9.0  # or similar version manager\n\n# Step 4: Initialize with v1.9.0\nterraform init\n\n# Step 5: Verify no unintended changes\nterraform plan\n\n# Step 6: Contact support if plan shows unexpected changes\n```\n\n---\n\n## Specific Migration Examples\n\n### Example 1: Complete S3 Backend Migration\n\n**Before (v1.9.0):**\n```hcl\nterraform {\n  required_version = \">= 1.9.0\"\n\n  backend \"s3\" {\n    bucket                       = \"terraform-state\"\n    key                          = \"prod/terraform.tfstate\"\n    region                       = \"us-east-1\"\n    dynamodb_table               = \"terraform-locks\"\n\n    role_arn                     = \"arn:aws:iam::123456789012:role/TerraformRole\"\n    session_name                 = \"terraform-prod\"\n    external_id                  = \"abc123xyz\"\n    assume_role_duration_seconds = 3600\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**After (v1.10.0):**\n```hcl\nterraform {\n  required_version = \">= 1.10.0\"\n\n  backend \"s3\" {\n    bucket         = \"terraform-state\"\n    key            = \"prod/terraform.tfstate\"\n    region         = \"us-east-1\"\n    dynamodb_table = \"terraform-locks\"\n\n    assume_role {\n      role_arn     = \"arn:aws:iam::123456789012:role/TerraformRole\"\n      session_name = \"terraform-prod\"\n      external_id  = \"abc123xyz\"\n      duration     = 3600\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### Example 2: Ephemeral Variable Usage\n\n```hcl\n# Provider configuration with ephemeral token\nvariable \"vault_token\" {\n  type      = string\n  ephemeral = true\n  default   = null\n}\n\nprovider \"vault\" {\n  address = \"https://vault.example.com\"\n  token   = var.vault_token\n}\n\n# Retrieve secrets using the ephemeral provider\ndata \"vault_generic_secret\" \"database\" {\n  path = \"secret/data/database\"\n}\n\n# Use secrets (but they won't be stored in state)\nresource \"aws_db_instance\" \"example\" {\n  master_username = data.vault_generic_secret.database.data[\"username\"]\n  master_password = data.vault_generic_secret.database.data[\"password\"]\n\n  # Note: These values ARE still stored in state as regular resource attributes\n  # Ephemeral only prevents the variable/data source from being persisted\n}\n```\n\n### Example 3: Moved Block with Keyword\n\n```hcl\n# If you had a resource with type \"local\" (hypothetically)\nresource \"random_pet\" \"pet\" {\n  keepers = {\n    example = \"value\"\n  }\n}\n\n# And previously had:\n# resource \"local\" \"old_name\" { }\n\n# In v1.10.0, update the moved block:\nmoved {\n  from = resource.local.old_name\n  to   = random_pet.pet\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Summary of Required Actions\n\n### Minimum Required Changes\n\n1. **S3 Backend Users**: Move IAM role attributes inside `assume_role` block\n2. **Moved Block Users**: Add `resource.` prefix if resource type matches keywords\n3. **All Users**: Run `terraform validate` and `terraform plan` before applying\n\n### Recommended Best Practices\n\n1. Use ephemeral variables for provider authentication instead of sensitive variables\n2. Review conditional expressions involving sensitive values\n3. Update bucket policies if enabling S3-native locking\n4. Document ephemeral value usage in module comments\n\n### Timeline for Deprecations\n\n- v1.10.0 (Nov 2024): IAM attributes removed, moved blocks require keyword safety, ephemeral values introduced\n- v1.11+ (Future): DynamoDB locking for S3 backend to be deprecated\n\n---\n\n## Getting Help\n\nIf you encounter issues during migration:\n\n1. **Check the official Terraform upgrade guide** - https://developer.hashicorp.com/terraform/language/upgrade-guides\n2. **Review error messages** - Terraform provides clear guidance on what changed\n3. **Test in non-production** - Always validate changes before production\n4. **Community forum** - https://discuss.hashicorp.com/c/terraform-core\n5. **GitHub issues** - https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform/issues\n\n---\n\n## Appendix: File Locations and Examples\n\n### Important Configuration Files\n\n- **Backend configuration**: Usually `backend.tf` or in `terraform {}` block of `main.tf`\n- **Moved blocks**: Can be in any `.tf` file, often in a dedicated `moved.tf`\n- **Variables**: Usually `variables.tf` or `variables/*.tf`\n- **Outputs**: Usually `outputs.tf`\n\n### Full Example: Multi-file Migration\n\n**backend.tf:**\n```hcl\nterraform {\n  backend \"s3\" {\n    bucket         = \"my-terraform-state\"\n    key            = \"prod/terraform.tfstate\"\n    region         = \"us-east-1\"\n    dynamodb_table = \"terraform-locks\"\n\n    assume_role {\n      role_arn = \"arn:aws:iam::123456789012:role/TerraformRole\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**main.tf:**\n```hcl\nterraform {\n  required_version = \">= 1.10.0\"\n  required_providers {\n    aws = {\n      source  = \"hashicorp/aws\"\n      version = \"~> 5.0\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nprovider \"aws\" {\n  region = \"us-east-1\"\n}\n\nresource \"aws_instance\" \"example\" {\n  ami           = \"ami-0c55b159cbfafe1f0\"\n  instance_type = \"t2.micro\"\n}\n```\n\n**moved.tf:**\n```hcl\n# Updated for v1.10.0\nmoved {\n  from = aws_instance.old_name\n  to   = aws_instance.example\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n**Last Updated**: November 2024\n**Terraform Versions**: v1.9.0 → v1.10.0\n**Status**: Complete for General Availability\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
